- **Readiness through the route** is decided here, for the reasons above. Reversible: the route is one raw handler over `status`.
- **`Overloaded` for drain refusals** is decided here. A `Draining` refusal would append tag 8 and change frame-vectors.json, and it would ask the client for the same action. Reversible until a client needs to tell the two apart.
- **The manifest from the build** is decided here, and extends D1. Reversible: the manifest is one file that one command writes.

## 14. Cold places under the platform host (new in v2.26)

A place run by the platform host (docs/spec/launch.md "Platform host and launcher") is **cold by default**: it runs only while it has work, and the host starts it again when work comes. This section is the wake contract between the two. The runner's idle stop and child report, the launcher's side of §14.3-§14.5 (`hirsel launcher`, launch.md "The launcher"), and the platform host (`core/programs/store/platform.hirsel`, `platform_main.hirsel`, `local.hirsel`) are **built**. In-process places run under the same host (§14.9).

The rule in one line: a place that a commit has given a future trigger is woken for it, and the host can always tell, from one durable row per place, whether it knows the place's next due time or must start the place to find out.

### 14.1 The wake row

The host's catalog has one row per authority group (one place) in its `wake` table:

```hirsel
pub type Wake: codec + send = Dirty(u64) | Clean(Option[u64])
pub type WakeRow: codec + send { wake: Wake, gen: u64 }
```

- `Dirty(g)`: the serving child with generation `g` is the **authoritative** child: it may hold, or may have held, the place's store, and the host does not know the place's triggers. The place must run to find out. (v2.27) `g` is at most `gen`; it is less while a cutover's candidate runs (§14.3).
- `Clean(next_due)`: no process holds the store, and `next_due` is the due time of the place's first pending trigger, in `at` units (`store.CallInfo.at`, wall milliseconds), or `None` when it has none that can run.
- `gen` counts the serving launches of the place. It only grows, and each serving launch, a cutover's candidate included, gets the next value.
- **A child's identity** (v2.27) is (group, incarnation, gen, role). `incarnation` is a value of a host-wide counter, written in the group's `groups` row when the group is added and never reused, so a group that is removed and added again under the same name shares no identity with its earlier self, and no late command or report of the earlier one can match it. `role` is the child's mode (launch.md "Child modes"). A `serving` and an `install` child hold the store, so each takes the next `gen` and the one-launch rule of §14.3; other children open no store and are keyed by their deploy attempt in place of `gen`. An install child (genesis, or an operator's standalone install) is the authoritative child while it runs, but it sends no clean report, so its end always leaves the row `Dirty(g)`; the host then starts the serving child at `gen + 1` as a relaunch, or retries the install under §14.5's backoff.
- **A new group.** Adding a group writes `Clean(None)` with `gen` 0 and a new `incarnation`, in the transaction that adds its `groups` row: its store does not exist yet, so it has no trigger. Its genesis is an operator launch and goes through `Dirty` like any other (§14.3).

Every change of a wake row is a transaction of the host's own store. The host waits for that commit to be durable before it acts on it (it launches nothing on a `Dirty` that could still be lost). So every argument below is about the order of the host's commits and of the facts the launcher reports, never about the order of tasks in one run (CONC-10, SEM-8 and CAP-8 promise none that this needs).

### 14.2 Capacity

The host's `config` row holds `max_places`, and the `wake` table keeps a count `dirty` of its `Dirty` rows, updated in the same transaction as every row change.

- **Check before `Dirty`.** A transaction that turns a `Clean` row into `Dirty` first reads `dirty`. If `dirty` ≥ `max_places`, it commits nothing and the start is refused `Full(Count(max_places))`. Otherwise it writes `Dirty(gen + 1)`, raises `gen` and `dirty`, and commits. A row that is already `Dirty` is re-marked `Dirty(gen + 1)` without a check, because `dirty` does not change, but only when the host launches a new serving child for it after the authoritative one has ended: a probe, or a relaunch after an arrival during a drain (§14.3). A cutover's candidate raises `gen` and leaves the row `Dirty(g)` until the cutover commits (§14.3). A wake of a group with an outstanding launch changes nothing (§14.3, the table).
- **The invariant.** `dirty` ≤ `max_places` holds in every committed state, because the host's store runs one writer and every transaction that raises `dirty` checks it.
- **Lowering the bound.** A `config` change that sets `max_places` below the current `dirty` is refused `Busy(dirty)`. The operator stops places first. So the invariant holds across changes, and no rule reads a historical bound.
- **No eviction.** A full host refuses or queues a start. It never stops a live place to make room.
- Each tenant's live-group quota is checked in the same transaction, the same way.
- **The host's own group** (v2.27) is not counted in `dirty` or in any tenant's quota, and it has no idle stop: it owns the wake timer and the queue, so it runs for as long as the platform does.

### 14.3 Waking

A place is woken by any of these:

- **Arrival.** The launcher reports `Arrived(group)` when a cold group's listener becomes readable. The connection waits in the backlog, and the new process accepts it from the inherited listener (launch.md "Routing").
- **Due time.** The host keeps an index of `Clean(Some(t))` rows by `t`. It waits for the earliest `t` with an absolute wall-time wait on its `Clock`, and wakes that place when the wall clock reads `t` or later. A trigger is due when the next commit's `at` is at least its due time (ENDSTATE T1), and that `at` is at least the wall clock, so the trigger is due when the woken head reads it. A wake that comes early because the clock stepped back is harmless: the head arms its timer for the trigger as at any open, and the idle rule (§14.4) keeps the place up until it runs.
- **Operator or deploy.** A deploy, an install, a genesis or an operator request to a cold place first wakes it. Every launch that opens the place's store goes through `Dirty`, so `next_due` is never stale after one of them.

**What a wake does, by the group's state.** The host knows a group's state from its own records and the launcher's events (§14.5), never from the child's phase. A serving launch is **outstanding** from the host's request until the launcher reports its end; between them the group is live, whether its child is not yet started, `Starting`, `Ready` or `Draining`.

| Group state (host) | Row | Arrival | Due time | Deploy or operator request | The authoritative child ends with a clean report for its `g` | It ends otherwise |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cold | `Clean(t)` | the check of §14.2, then `Dirty(gen + 1)` and a launch; on `Full` queued (below) | the same | the check, then wake first and act; on `Full` refused `Full`, which the caller may retry | (no child) | (no child) |
| Live, one outstanding launch `g` | `Dirty(g)` | coalesced: the launcher watches a listener only while its group has no outstanding serving launch | coalesced: the index holds `Clean` rows only, and the live head arms its own | acts on the live child; a deploy starts a cutover | `Clean(next_due)`, unless the end report says the listener is readable: then `Dirty(gen + 1)` and a relaunch | stays `Dirty(g)`; a probe at `gen + 1` (§14.5, backoff) |
| Cutover, OLD `g` and candidate `c` | `Dirty(g)` | not reported | none | refused `Busy` until the cutover ends | held until the cutover decides (below) | held until the cutover decides |
| Failing (§14.5), no child | `Dirty(g)` | coalesced into the scheduled retry | the same | an operator retry starts it now; a deploy; a retirement (§14.5) | (no child) | (no child) |
| Retiring (§14.5) | `Dirty(g)` | dropped | dropped | refused `Busy` | applied, then retirement goes on | applied, then retirement goes on |

**One serving launch at a time (v2.27).** A group has at most one outstanding serving launch, and two during a cutover: OLD and one candidate. Every wake cause of a group with an outstanding launch is coalesced into it and changes nothing. A launch request is keyed by the child's identity (§14.1). A repeated request with the same key is answered with that child's current state and starts nothing, so a host that is unsure whether its request arrived may send it again. The launcher enforces the same bound: it refuses a serving launch for a (group, incarnation) that has a serving child or an accepted start, unless the request is a candidate that names that child's `g` and no other candidate exists. So two serving launches of one group are never requested by mistake, and STORE's `LOCK` is the last check, not the only one. On objects@1 there is no such last check: a later claim deposes the earlier holder, which then stops on `Fenced` (§2.1). So this rule is the guard there, and a violation costs a deposed head and its in-flight calls, never two heads that both commit.

**Cutover commit and abort (v2.27).** A deploy launches NEW as a candidate at `c = gen + 1`, raising `gen`, while the row stays `Dirty(g)` naming OLD. NEW is prestarted with the deploy's activation `v` as its selection. OLD's head does the cutover: under its write pause it imports the prepared target and installs `v` under `LOCK` (on objects@1, by the activation CHECKPOINT's head write, STORE §12.7), and it answers the host `Installed(v)`, `Drift` or `Refused` (launch.md "Hot deploy"). Until the cutover decides, OLD is the authoritative child, and the host applies no report of NEW's.
- **Preparing.** Before any of this the deploy is `Preparing`. The host commits a row naming the attempt, NEW's assembly and image digest, and OLD's `g`, then releases the attempt's reservation. In order, it runs OLD's describe child, then NEW's preview child on the exact bytes of OLD's exported list. It checks that the preview names NEW's image digest, the verified target and `n_place`, a 32-byte release, and one answer per old entry. Only then does it send OLD `HostExport`. On `PlaceExported(head, name)` with a valid name it runs NEW's prepare child on that export. After that child exits 0 it sends OLD `HostImport` with NEW's exact `Describe` bytes, the release and the image digest. On `PlaceImported(digest)` one transaction re-checks that the row is still `Preparing` for this attempt under `Dirty(g)`. It allocates `c`, records `v = head + 1`, the prepared digest and the release, and makes the row `Pending`. On objects@1 `PlaceImported` also carries OLD's pair, `store.claim_of` on OLD's Db (fixed for OLD's open, STORE §12.3), and the row records it with the `Describe` digests of OLD (from its describe child) and NEW (from the preview) (v2.36); only then do the candidate's `Start` and the cutover go out. A failure at any step (a refused or failed helper, a helper that ends without its answer, a check, `PlaceRefused`, OLD's end) stops the helper. Once the export was asked for, it also sends OLD `HostAbort`. The row stays `Preparing` until OLD answers `PlaceAborted` or ends and the helper has ended, and only then records `Failed`. A new session finds a `Preparing` row with no cutover sent. If OLD runs, the session sends it `HostAbort` and records `Failed(interrupted before the cutover)` on its answer; if OLD is gone, it records that failure at once. A `Preparing` deploy keeps the group `Busy` for another deploy. Recovery of a drift retry
sends `HostAbort(retry, Some(original))`. OLD releases the export and write pause matching
either attempt and answers `PlaceAborted` after applying the abort.
- **Commit.** OLD's head answers `Installed(v)`. The host writes `Dirty(c)` and the deploy row's outcome in one transaction. OLD then retires and closes the store, and NEW opens it at `v`. From then on OLD's reports name `g` and change nothing, and an end of OLD that was held is dropped.
- **An unknown install (v2.34, objects@1).** If OLD's install returns `Store(Fenced(e))` or `Store(Undecided)`, the activation may or may not have applied (STORE §12.7). OLD's head answers nothing and ends abnormally (§2.1), the launcher reports `Uncertain`, which counts as OLD's end, and the deploy takes the **Undecided** path below. The attempt is decided at `v` (the exported main head + 1 the host recorded, never OLD's activation + 1) by comparing `releases[v].prepared` with this attempt's prepared digest (never `releases[v].digest`, which is the image digest; STORE v2.49 §12.7), from one capture: a probe's open, or after a refused open the probe's follower read below. OLD never answers `Refused` for it.
- **Abort.** OLD's head answers `Refused` (only for a failure before its activation head write, STORE §12.7), or `Drift` with no retry left. The first `Drift` of a deploy is its one retry: OLD keeps its write pause, the deploy row returns to `Preparing` at a fresh attempt with the original attempt recorded, and OLD exports again as a retry under that pause; the candidate is discarded as below, and once OLD keeps the retry's import and the discarded candidate has ended, a new one starts at the next counter value (launch.md "Hot deploy"). The store's current activation is still OLD's, and `v` was never installed. The host sends `AbortCutover`, records the failure in the deploy row, and discards the candidate; the row stays `Dirty(g)`. A discarded candidate cannot have changed the store: its selection names `v`, so if it ever takes `LOCK` it is refused `NotCurrent` before its handles run (§12.1, `Activating`). So OLD's later clean report is still exact, and the candidate's end clears nothing and restarts nothing; it is not OLD's end.
- **Undecided.** OLD's head gave no answer (OLD ended, or the session was lost in the pause). If the candidate reports that it opened the store at `v` with the release the preview reported **and** `releases[v].prepared` equal to the attempt's prepared digest (v2.35), that proves the install, and the cutover commits. An open at `v` whose `prepared` differs proves that this attempt did not install: the deploy is recorded failed (`not installed after recovery`) with both roots kept, and the group's pair follows the store's activation as a `PlaceCurrent` does. Otherwise the host does not guess: both ends are discarded, the row stays `Dirty(g)`, and the deploy is `Undecided` with both roots kept. The group is probed at a new generation with NEW's assembly at `v` first (launch.md "When the host starts"). Every probe of an Undecided deploy names the attempt's `v` as its **witness version**, apart from its selection.
  - **A probe that opens** decides: its claim changed the pair, so `releases[v]` present with the attempt's digest is Installed, and present with another `prepared` or absent is failed. An open at `v` commits NEW as above; an open at another activation records the outcome and serves that activation.
  - **The probe's read (v2.36, objects@1).** A probe refused `NotCurrent` takes, before it exits, STORE §12.7's follower read of one capture: `follower` with `o.expect` set to the store ID the host recorded, `fresh`, then `follower_head` and `releases[witness]`. Its `PlaceCurrent` carries the store ID, HEAD `activation` a, HEAD `image`, the pair and the row's `prepared` or "absent". A failed read (`Remote`, `Full`, `Foreign`, `Reclaimed`, `Behind`) reports `PlaceCurrent` with no read, which decides nothing and is retried under the ordinary backoff.
  - **The outcome**, from the read by STORE §12.7, in one transaction: the attempt's digest is Installed; another `prepared`, or absent under another pair than OLD's recorded pair, is failed (`not installed after recovery`). Absent under OLD's pair stays Undecided: OLD's delayed head write can still land, so the next probe selects OLD's pair at OLD's activation, and its open (a claim, which ends that write for good) or its read decides. Row `v` decides even when a is above `v`.
  - **What serves**, recorded in the same transaction as a decided outcome and never derived from it: activation a, with whichever of OLD's and NEW's assemblies has the `Describe` digest equal to the read's image. So an Installed attempt whose store has since moved to `v + 1` serves `v + 1`, never `v`, and a failed one never selects OLD's activation once another install moved the head. If neither matches (another install's `Describe`, such as another `n_place`), the recorded activation is unchanged and the group is failing with the cause `NotCurrent: the store is at activation a under another image`, until an operator deploy or install. The next start's open (§12.1, STORE §12.1) stays the guard either way.
  - **Roots.** The selected assembly is current. The other is retained when the deploy installed or when NEW was selected. A failed deploy whose store serves OLD leaves NEW no root. With no assembly selected, OLD stays current and NEW retained.
  - On log@1 the read is not taken: a refused probe reports `PlaceCurrent(a)` as before (open item, STORE CHANGES-v2.49).
- **Across sessions.** The deploy row is durable before the candidate's `Start` and the cutover command, and it holds that command's arguments: the attempt, the image digest, OLD's `g` and the candidate's `c`. Each new session's inventory resumes every row still pending. With the candidate in the inventory, the host sends the identical cutover command again: the launcher forwards it if it never did, stays silent while OLD holds it, and otherwise returns OLD's kept answer, or `Uncertain` if OLD ended first, which counts as OLD's end. With OLD running and no candidate, the candidate's `Start` goes out first, then the command. While the launcher holds OLD's answer, the ends of OLD and the candidate are recorded and decide nothing until that answer is applied. With neither child and no held answer, nothing proves the install: the outcome is `Undecided` and the group is probed. OLD therefore installs at most once per attempt whatever the host loses. The host sends `CutoverAck` only after its commit, abort or undecided outcome is durable, and acknowledges any held cutover that names no pending row. A launcher lost after OLD installed `v` leaves neither child nor held answer: the probe of NEW at `v` opens and commits NEW, with no cutover sent again; one lost before the install gets `PlaceCurrent` from that probe and the open of OLD's pair records the failure. The platform harness checks each loss point in `cutover_survives_host_loss`, asserting each recovery Start's generation, activation and assembly, and the catalog's root after the probe's answer. After each outcome a collection through the host keeps the roots the outcome left (above: the selected assembly and the retained one), an Undecided or Preparing deploy keeps both, and a failed deploy's NEW is no root unless the store's image is NEW's (launch.md "Collect"). The harness also checks the moved-activation histories of STORE L54 (v2.36): after a host restart, the read decides each, and a collection keeps the hand-derived roots.

**Event order (v2.27).** The launcher's events reach the host as one ordered stream per control session (§14.5), and the host applies the events of one group in stream order; it may interleave groups. When a serving child ends, the launcher first samples its listener for readability, then sends the child's `End` with that answer in it, and only then starts its level-triggered watch of the listener. So an `Arrived` for the group can only follow that `End` in the stream, and the host never takes an `Arrived` while it still counts the ended child as outstanding. Each inventory (§14.5) repeats every readable cold listener, so a readable listener is reported again after a reconnect. No order across groups is needed.

**An arrival during a drain.** A drain closes admission (§12.1; an idle stop has already closed it at its confirmation, §14.4), so a connection that arrives after admission closes waits in the listener's backlog, which the launcher still holds. It is in the `End` report or a later `Arrived`, and the next process answers it. Neither waits behind a `Clean` row. A connection that arrives before admission closes may be answered by the draining child.

**Queued wakes (v2.27).** A wake runs the check of §14.2. On success the host launches the serving child with the selected activation, and the launcher tags the child with its identity. On `Full`, a due time or an arrival is queued in the host's memory, one entry per group with its earliest cause, in (time of that cause, group name) order. An operator or deploy request is never queued: it is refused `Full`, and the caller retries it, so the queue holds only causes the host can rebuild.
- The host retries the queue on every change that can make room: a row leaving `Dirty`, a raised `max_places`, a raised tenant quota, and a retirement. It tries each queued group in order, independently: one that still does not fit (its tenant is still full) stays queued and does not block the ones after it.
- The due-time wait always follows the minimum of the index of `Clean(Some(t))` rows that are not queued. It is recomputed whenever that minimum changes: an insert below it, an insert into an empty index, or a removal. The host's driver owns this wait and the backoff wait and re-arms both after every input it applies (launch.md "Running the host"), so a minimum that changes after start is never missed. A row that is overdue and queued has left the wait, so it cannot spin the timer or hold back a later row of another group.
- After a host restart the queue is rebuilt from what is durable or observable: each row of the due index whose `t` has passed, and each readable cold listener of the inventory. Nothing else was in it.
- The trigger then runs late, which ENDSTATE T2 already allows: it runs once, and sees how late it is from its own `at`. The host reports each queued wake and its age in its status, so a lateness is never hidden.

### 14.4 The idle stop and the clean report

**Idle (extends §12.1).** `PlaceConfig` gains `idle: Option[u64]`, an idle time in milliseconds; `Some(0)` is refused `Missing("idle")`. With `Some(i)`, `operate` stops the place itself when it is idle for `i`. A part is **idle** when:
- S holds no session;
- H holds no worker and no FIFO entry, its trigger drive has no active run, and it holds no export lease (§5 "Prepared deployment"; a hot deploy's export keeps its place up until the cutover or the attempt ends);
- H has no pending trigger due before `at` + `i`, where `at` is `store.next_call_info(db).at`, among the rows `triggers.next_pending` returns: branch 0, not active, and not parked unless its cancel is requested (v2.27; a parked row is skipped as at dispatch, and a cancelling one is finished at its due time).

**The idle check (v2.27).** H and S send `operate` a message each time their part becomes idle or busy. `operate` arms a deadline `i` after both have reported idle. A busy report cancels the deadline. These messages are only hints: a part can start work after it sent its last one. When the deadline expires, `operate` asks the parts themselves, S first and then H:
1. S answers from its own state. If it holds no session, it interrupts A's pending accept, waits until A has parked before its next accept, and confirms. From then on a connection waits in the listener's backlog, which the next process accepts (launch.md "Routing"). A connection that A took before it parked is a session, and S answers busy.
2. After S confirms, `operate` asks H. H answers after it has handled every input it took before the ask, from its own state, by the rule above. If it is idle it confirms and then admits nothing until `operate` answers: it takes no request, starts no trigger attempt and grants no export lease. A trigger start or a lease that H made before the ask makes it answer busy.
3. When both confirm, `operate` takes the stop as `StopRequested`, by the path of §12.1 "Draining". Admission is already closed: A stays parked and is dropped when admission closes, so the place accepts no connection after S confirmed, and a connection in the backlog is answered by the next process (§14.3). H leaves its parked state only to drain.
4. When either answers busy, `operate` releases the part that confirmed, resets the interval and keeps serving.

So an idle stop never cuts a run or a lease that started before it was decided, and nothing starts after it. An active granted run therefore keeps its place up. Without this rule, a drain would cut the run and the next start would run attempt n + 1, so a cold place would churn its attempts.

**Where `idle` comes from.** The platform host sets an idle time for every serving launch of a platform place. The launcher starts the place as a **wake launch**: it puts `--hirsel-wake --hirsel-idle=<ms>`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, with a nonzero idle time, in the runtime prefix, before `--hirsel-activation`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and the program's arguments, and `runner.main` passes that time to `operate` in place of `PlaceConfig.idle`. An idle time of 0 is refused as `Some(0)` is. Every other launch of `runner.main`, and the fixture, use `PlaceConfig.idle` as given, which is `None` unless the program sets it.

**Unload (v2.31).** `platform.unload(name)` asks a group's live serving child to stop. It is refused `Unknown` for no group, `Retiring` for a group being retired, `Busy` while a cutover holds a candidate, and `Missing("live")` when no child runs. Otherwise the host sends `Stop` to the serving child of the current incarnation and generation, which drains as an idle stop does (§12.1 "Draining"). The end is applied by the clean-report rule below: a clean `Stopped(next_due)` writes `Clean(next_due)`, any other end leaves `Dirty` and probes. An arrival during the drain relaunches at gen + 1 (§14.3, L46). So an unload changes when the place runs, never whether a due trigger runs.

**The clean report (extends §12.1).** A head that ends on a failed `durable` or on a store health other than `Ok` sends none (§2.1). `Ended` becomes `Stopped(Option[u64]) | Unstarted | Forced(u64)`. On the drain's finish (§12.1 step 6), after the last commit is durable and before `store.close`, H reads `triggers.next_pending` on its last snapshot with no active run. It sends that row's due time, or `None`, to `operate` in its last message, and `operate` returns `Ok(Stopped(next_due))`. A parked trigger is skipped unless its cancel is requested, as at dispatch: only a release or a cancel, each a transaction through an entry and so a wake, can make it runnable. A run that a drain cut is pending again, so its due time is in the past and the place is woken at once. A head that runs no trigger drive (§12.1: no row and no entry) returns `Stopped(None)`.

`runner.main` started as a wake launch writes `next_due` on inherited descriptor 5, the launcher's private report descriptor (the listener and the control socket stay on descriptors 3 and 4), when it gets `Stopped(next_due)`, as one value-codec `Option[u64]`, and exits 0. It writes nothing for `Unstarted`, `Forced` or a `StartError`. The launcher forwards the report with the child's `gen` and its exit status.

**Only a clean `Stopped` writes `Clean`.** The host writes `Clean(next_due)` in one transaction that requires the report to come from a `serving` child of the group's current incarnation, the row to be `Dirty(g)` with the report's `gen` equal to `g`, no candidate to be outstanding, the exit status to be 0 and the report to be present, and it lowers `dirty`. Any other end leaves the row `Dirty(gen)`: a crash, a kill, `Unstarted`, `Forced`, a `StartError`, an exit 0 with no report, a report from an older generation, or a lost report. The generation fence matters at a cutover: once NEW has committed, the row names NEW, so OLD's later report names the old `g` and changes nothing; before that, OLD's report is held (§14.3).

### 14.5 Crashes, restarts and probes

The host durably records the store ID from the first accepted `PlaceOpened` for each group
incarnation. A hosted runner waits for `HostStoreAccepted` before serving. The launcher
repeats an unaccepted `PlaceOpened` after the next session's inventory. Subsequent serving starts carry that expected ID in their private launch
metadata. After opening the store under LOCK, the runner compares the ID before it
prepares the activation, opens application handles, or serves. On objects@1 it passes the expected ID as `o.expect`, so the store refuses another store with `Foreign`, and an empty namespace with `NotFound`, before any write (STORE §12.1); the runner reports both as below. On a mismatch it sends
`PlaceStoreMismatch(expected_hi, expected_lo, actual_hi, actual_lo)` and exits. The
launcher logs the group and both IDs. The host rejects a `PlaceOpened` with a different
store ID as recovery evidence.

**The control session (v2.27).** The host talks to the launcher over one control session at a time, on the launcher's control socket (launch.md "One control socket").
- A session starts with the host's `Hello`. The launcher first ends any earlier session: it stops reading the old connection, and each request of it that it had not acted on is discarded, never acted on later. Only then does it answer.
- Its answer is the **inventory**, taken in the one loop in which the launcher changes its state: every child it has, by identity (§14.1) and with its state (accepted and not yet started, started, or ended with an end the host has not acknowledged, with the end's report); every cold listener that is readable; and no collection fence (a new session drops it, launch.md "Collect").
- Every event the launcher sends afterwards follows the inventory in the same stream. Each state change happens in that one loop, so each is either in the inventory or after it, never both and never neither. The launcher keeps an ended child in the inventory until the host acknowledges its end, so an end is not lost with a session.
- The inventory also lists each cutover command the launcher forwarded to an OLD child that the host has not acknowledged, keyed by (OLD child, attempt). The launcher keeps OLD's first answer, or `Uncertain` if OLD ended without one, until `CutoverAck`. A re-sent identical cutover command for that key is answered with the kept reply as `CutoverResult`, never forwarded again, and a different command for the same key is refused. So a host that lost a cutover's reply with its session learns the decided answer instead of repeating the install.
- A new session ends every reservation and every running admission of the old one. The host has already made each attempt whose pending row is durable a root through that row, and it abandons each attempt whose row is not ("When the host starts"); no collection runs before that reconciliation.
- A child in the inventory that matches no row (an old incarnation, a removed group, or a generation that is neither the authoritative child nor a candidate of a pending deploy) is stopped by its identity, and nothing it reports is applied.
- A launch request that the host sent in an earlier session and that is not in the inventory will never run. So the host may start a new generation without fearing a late start of an older one: a command is fenced at the launcher before its child can open the store.

**A host restart (the launcher lives).** Only the host's process ended, and the launcher starts it again as its first group: at once the first time, then after a doubling delay from 1 s up to 5 min, reset by a 5 min run, and never after status 64 or 74 (launch.md "Starting the host"). Live groups keep serving, and cold listeners keep their backlogs. The host reconciles in this order:
1. It opens a session and takes the inventory.
2. It **adopts** each serving child whose (incarnation, `gen`) is the row's authoritative child: the group is live, nothing is interrupted, and the child's later end and report are applied as usual. It acknowledges each ended child and applies its end as §14.3 and §14.4 say.
3. It reconciles each pending deploy (launch.md "When the host starts"). A candidate of that deploy found in the inventory is adopted and its cutover goes on (§14.3).
4. Each `Dirty` row that has no adopted child and no accepted start is a **probe**. Each readable listener and each passed due time of a `Clean` row is a wake (§14.3, "Queued wakes").

**A launcher loss.** The launcher's process ended, so every listener it held is closed and its children lost their parent. The host is one of them: it is the launcher's first group, so it is ended with the rest and started again by the new launcher, and it never reconnects across a launcher loss.
- The new launcher first **fences** the old one: it takes the launcher lock, an OS lock on a file in its state directory that a launcher holds for its whole life, and the lock is free only once no earlier launcher process is alive.
- It then finds every container it owns (each named by the child's identity under its delegated subtree), kills every process in each, waits until each is empty, and removes staging. Only then does it answer `Hello`, with an inventory that has no child. So no contained child survives a launcher loss.
- An `uncontained` child can survive it (EXEC-6 gives only best-effort death). It may still hold its store's `LOCK`, so its group's probe is refused `Held` and the group is failing until it dies. On objects@1 there is no `Held`: a probe whose opening checks pass claims and deposes the survivor, whose next head write or poll ends it on `Fenced` (§2.1). A probe refused by those checks writes nothing and deposes no one. That is the price of the label, and the host's status names the group.
- **Listeners.** A cold listener's backlog is lost with its only holder. The no-loss promise of launch.md "Routing" holds across a child's end and a host restart only. The new launcher binds each group's registered address again as the host lists it. A bind refused because a survivor still holds the address makes that group failing, with the error, until the address is free.
- The host then reconciles as for a host restart, with an empty inventory: every `Dirty` row is a probe.

**Genesis (v2.32).** A group added for its genesis has activation 0 in its `groups` row. Its first serving start selects `Current(0)`, installs the store's first activation `v` under `LOCK` (§12.1 `Activating`), and the host records `v` with the store ID when it applies that start's `PlaceOpened` (above). A crash in between, whether the child's, the host's or the launcher's, leaves the store at `v` and the group at 0. Every later start at 0 is then refused `NotCurrent(Active(v))`, which the child reports as `PlaceCurrent(v)`. The host handles that answer as follows:
- **Recorded activation 0, no cutover.** The host records `v` for the group in one transaction that re-checks the incarnation, the recorded 0 and that the group is not retiring. The child's end then relaunches the group at `gen + 1` with `v` at once, outside backoff, as an Undecided probe's answer does.
- **The proof.** That start compares the store's `Describe` with its own before handles (§12.1), and only its `PlaceOpened` records the store ID. So a store of the same image opens and serves. A store that another image activated refuses this start too.
- **Any other `PlaceCurrent`** (the recorded activation is not 0, or the answer names 0) changes nothing, except a read reported by a probe of an Undecided deploy, which §14.3 applies. It is kept as the start's cause, and the start's end is unsuccessful with that cause as the group's `last` error (`NotCurrent: the store is at activation v under another image`, or `…, the host selected w`).

The adoption is one-shot: a group with a recorded activation never adopts again, so the relaunch outside backoff happens at most once per incarnation. A host restart after the adoption's commit finds the activation already recorded and starts at `v`.

The window's crash points and the row after each, with no operator or deploy input:
- Dies before the install: the store is empty, and the probe at 0 runs genesis.
- Dies after the install and before `PlaceOpened` is applied (with or without a host restart): the probe at 0 is answered `PlaceCurrent(v)` and records `v`, and the next start at `v` opens.
- The host restarts after the adoption's commit and before it applies the probe's end: the restart's start is at `v`.
- Dies after `PlaceOpened` is applied: the probe is at `v`.

In each case the group serves again at `v`. No wake is lost: the row stays `Dirty` throughout, so it is probed. No start installs a second activation, because only a start at 0 on an empty store installs one. The generation fence and the clean-report rule are unchanged.

**Probes.** A probe is started without a capacity check, since its row is already counted. It recovers the store, arms its triggers at open (one already past due runs at once), runs until it is idle, and writes `Clean` by the clean report. By the invariant, a restart probes at most `max_places` places, and it never scans `Clean` rows.

**Failing and backoff (v2.27).** A start is **unsuccessful** when it ends with a `StartError`, is refused `Held`, or its child ends without a clean report before it has been `Ready` for 5 min (a crash, a kill, `Forced`, an exit with no report). The first unsuccessful start is retried at once; each further one waits a doubling delay from 1 s up to 5 min. A clean report, or 5 min in `Ready`, resets the count. While a retry waits, the group is **failing**: its row stays `Dirty(g)`, so it keeps its slot, its arrivals and due times are coalesced into the scheduled retry, and the host's status lists it with the last error: the start's own refusal when it gave one ("Genesis" above), and otherwise the child's exit status. After `Forced` its store is at a crash cut, and the next open recovers it (§12.1 "At D"). An operator retry starts it now; a deploy or a retirement ends the failing state.

**Liveness.** A due trigger runs once its group can get a slot and a start succeeds. While every slot is held by live or failing groups, the no-eviction rule (§14.2) promises no firing time; the queued wake and its age are in the host's status, and the operator can raise `max_places` or retire a group.

**Removing a group (v2.27).** A group whose row is `Clean` is removed in one transaction, and the launcher then closes its listener. A group whose row is `Dirty`, failing or not, is **retired**:
1. The host commits a retiring mark in its `groups` row. From then on it launches nothing for the group and drops its wakes. A host restart that finds the mark goes on from step 2.
2. It asks the launcher `Retire(group, incarnation)`. The launcher refuses every later launch of that incarnation, stops each of its children, and answers `Retired` only when no process of the group remains: for `contained`, every container is empty; for `uncontained`, the direct child has exited and the store's `LOCK` can be taken.
3. The host then removes the group's rows, lowers `dirty` and releases its tenant slot in one transaction, and records the store's disposition that the operator's request named: `keep`, where the store directory stays, detached from every group, and its pending triggers never run; or `delete`. The launcher closes the listener.

Retirement never writes `Clean` and runs no trigger. Until `Retired` is answered, the group keeps its slot.

### 14.6 Deploys and pending triggers

A deploy runs on a live place, so its row is `Dirty` for the whole deploy, NEW runs as a candidate at the next generation, and the row names NEW only once the cutover commits (§14.3).

**The prepare scan** is launch.md "Preparing the pending trigger report". It runs in the prepare child against the staged catalog, after `migrate`. `PrepareConfig` gains `triggers: triggers.Entries`, the same list the place passes in `TriggerConfig`. The scan classifies each pending row with the head's own dispatch code, as the manifest preview uses `answer_want` (§12.2): `trigger_key` is the one function that says whether NEW has the row's entry, and `triggers.start`'s revision comparison says whether a started granted run is held. So the scan cannot disagree with what NEW's head does. Its classes are tried in `advance_trigger`'s order, and a row takes the first that applies (v2.27):
1. `cancelling`: its cancel is requested, parked or not. The head finishes a cancel at its due time before it looks at the entry.
2. `held`: a granted row that is parked, or whose attempt is at least 1 **and** whose revision stamp differs from NEW's launch image digest. `triggers.start` parks the second kind with a `Revision` hold before the head looks for the entry. A parked `Request` hold stays parked even when the digests are equal. A run that a release has already moved to NEW's stamp is not held.
3. `gone`: NEW's entry list does not allow its entry, or no registered entry of NEW has its kind, path and argument schema (`trigger_key`). A started granted run under an equal digest lands here when its entry is removed: `triggers.start` raises its attempt, and the head consumes it `Gone`.
4. `changed`: an allowed entry whose `revision` differs from OLD's.
5. `unchanged`: every other row.

Two recipes may differ in `n_place` alone and share one image digest, so NEW's digest can equal a started run's stamp; the scan compares the stamp, never assumes it differs. OLD's revisions come from the export's `current` activation row. The report is exact, and one that does not fit the prepare child's bound fails prepare with `Limit(triggers)`.

**The image-digest guard stays.** A granted trigger's revision stamp is the launch image digest (`make_trigger_drive`), and a start of attempt ≥ 2 under another digest parks the run with a `Revision` hold. `Exported.revision` does not cover `handles` (a table name, a binding, a key codec, an index extractor), so a revision-keyed guard would let a started run resume against changed handles. The prepare scan classifies these runs as `held`.

### 14.7 Laws

L40 runs in the runner's prepare harness and L44 in the runner's place harness, since each needs one place and no host. Every other law runs in a platform host harness (lane P3 in platform-host-spec-1741/REPORT.md) over places that use triggers, with the launcher's child launches faked only where the law says so. The oracle of each is the rule cited. L46's launcher half, where a child's end is sent after its listener's exit sample and before the watch resumes, and an inventory repeats a readable listener, is built in the launcher's own harness (crates/hirsel-core-driver/src/launcher/laws.rs, `end_samples_listener_before_watch`).

| Law | Wrong implementation it catches | Oracle |
|---|---|---|
| L39 `wake_due_after_unload`: a place commits a trigger due at t + 2 s and goes idle; its process ends with `Stopped(Some(t + 2000))`; with no arrival, the trigger's outcome is committed after wall time t + 2 s. (v2.27) While the wait is armed for a place due at t + 60 s, and again while the index is empty, a second place goes `Clean(Some(t + 2000))`; its trigger runs after t + 2 s, not at t + 60 s | the host keeps §13.5's no-wake rule, or writes `Clean(None)`, so the trigger never runs; or the wait is armed only at start, so an earlier deadline waits for the old one | §14.3, §14.4 |
| L40 `trigger_scan_classes` (v2.27): **(a)** under a NEW image whose digest differs from OLD's, one OLD store holds a pending transact trigger for an entry NEW removes, a started granted run, a started granted run whose entry NEW removes, a cancelling row for an entry NEW removes, a parked row whose cancel is requested, one for an entry whose body NEW changes, and one for an unchanged entry; the scan reports `gone`, `held` (two), `cancelling` (two), `changed` and `unchanged`; NEW's head then consumes the first with `Gone`, parks both started runs with a `Revision` hold, and finishes both cancels. **(b)** Under a NEW recipe that differs in `n_place` only, so with an equal digest: a started granted run is `unchanged` and runs attempt 2; one whose entry NEW removes is `gone` and is consumed `Gone`; a run parked by a `Request` hold is `held` and stays parked. **(c)** NEW lists no trigger entry at all: OLD's one future transact row is `gone`, and at its due time NEW's head, served by the normal serving path, commits its terminal `Gone` | the scan reads OLD's registry or the pre-migration catalog, compares revisions for granted runs, assumes NEW's digest differs, tries `gone` before `held` or `cancelling`, or the head runs no drive under an empty list and strands the row | §12.1, §14.6, launch.md, `advance_trigger` |
| L41 `wake_dirty_capacity`: with `max_places` 2, two places are live and a third's due time comes; it is not started, its row stays `Clean`, and it starts when one of the two writes `Clean`. (v2.27) With both still live, raising `max_places` to 3 starts it, and so does raising a full tenant's quota in a second case; with the third queued, a host restart rebuilds the wake from the due index and the trigger runs; a queued place of a full tenant does not hold back a due place of another; an operator request that finds no slot is answered `Full` | the host writes `Dirty` before the check, so a restart probes 3 > `max_places` places; or it retries only when a row leaves `Dirty`, or loses the queue with its memory | §14.2, §14.3 |
| L42 `wake_only_clean_stop`: a child killed after its drain began, a child that ends `Forced`, and a child that exits 0 without a report each leave the row `Dirty`; each is started again as a probe, and so is each one left `Dirty` across a host restart, and its due trigger runs. (v2.27) A child that crashes 1 s after `Ready` on every start is restarted at once, then after 1 s, 2 s, 4 s; arrivals meanwhile start nothing extra; a clean report resets the delay | the host writes `Clean` on any exit 0 or on `Draining`; or it restarts a post-start crash at once every time, outside the backoff | §14.4, §14.5 |
| L43 `wake_generation_fence`: during a cutover, OLD's clean report arrives after NEW has committed; the row stays `Dirty(c)`, and NEW's idle stop then writes `Clean`. (v2.27) **Refused cutover:** NEW's install is refused while OLD serves; the row stays `Dirty(g)`, NEW's end changes nothing and starts nothing, and OLD's later clean report writes `Clean`. **Lost acknowledgement:** the host commits `Dirty(5)` and sends the launch, and the session drops before its answer; in a second case before the launcher read it. After `Hello`, the first case adopts child 5 and the second probes at 6, and in neither does a child 5 start after 6 or does a report of 5 clear the row. **Re-added group:** a group removed and added again under its name ignores the old incarnation's late report | a report is accepted without its identity or role, so the host believes the place stopped while NEW serves; a discarded candidate's end probes against OLD; or a lost launch runs after recovery started its successor | §14.3, §14.5 |
| L44 `idle_keeps_runs` (v2.27): a granted run that waits on its outside step for 3 × `idle` keeps its place up, and records one attempt; and with H and S both reported idle, a granted attempt (and in a second case an export lease) that H starts just before the idle deadline that would stop the place makes H answer busy, so the place keeps serving and the run records one attempt | idle counts sessions only, or `operate` stops on its own record of H's and S's last messages, so the place drains, the run is cut, and attempt 2 runs | §14.4 |
| L45 `wake_probe_bound`: after `max_places` places are left `Dirty` by kills and the host restarts with 10,000 `Clean` rows, it starts exactly the `Dirty` ones as recovery probes, counted apart from the ordinary due wakes it also makes, and lowering `max_places` below them is refused `Busy`. (v2.27) **Host-only restart:** G serves at `Dirty(7)` and only the host is killed; G keeps serving and is adopted, no probe 8 starts, and G's clean report for 7 writes `Clean`. **Retirement:** with `max_places` 1, A fails every start and B's due wake is queued; retiring A answers `Retired`, frees the slot, and B starts | the host probes every row, or accepts a lower bound and breaks the invariant; it probes live children after a host restart; or a failing group can never give up its slot | §14.2, §14.5 |
| L46 `wake_arrival_during_drain`: a place's idle stop is confirmed, so admission is closed; a client then connects, and in a second case connects just after the launcher's exit sample; each connection is answered by the next process, and the row goes `Dirty(gen)` → `Dirty(gen + 1)` → `Clean` with no `Clean` between; the host applies `End` before the `Arrived` that follows it, and a reconnect between them reports the listener readable in the inventory | the launcher reports the end before it checks the listener, the host writes `Clean`, or the host applies `Arrived` while it still counts the child live and drops it, so the connection waits until the next due time or forever | §14.3 |
| L47 `trigger_snapshot_fenced` (v2.29): a granted run's first attempt is cut at its first boundary; after reopen, attempt 2 is waiting at its boundary. A `Snap` for attempt 1 is refused `StaleAttempt`, and attempt 2's snapshot returns the row the test wrote in the trigger's arming transaction. The run then completes once | the head answers `Snap` before the fence, from a stale attempt, or with a snapshot older than the last commit | §12.1, `answer_trigger` |
| L48 `deploy_admission_policy`: the host relays a deploy's admission and decides its approvals from the verified summary. With the group's memory 100, root limits 60 (NEW) and 50 (OLD) are `Refused(Memory)` and released, with no Assemble, no Preparing row and no helper; 50 and 50 assemble. A nonempty grant diff (NEW adds `Net=tcp:example.org:443`) is released before Assemble and holds the request: its owner is shown `AwaitDecision` with that diff, the hold survives the owner's hang-up, a Decide on another diff is `Denied`, and a Decide on exactly that diff repeats the admission under a fresh attempt and assembles; a refused diff fails the request `Denied`, and a later Begin of the group fails a held request `Denied`. Nonempty approval text assembles only after the operator confirms the SHA-256 of exactly that text on the connection that owns the request, and a different hash is `Denied` and released; the assembled deploy is Preparing, its reservation released and OLD's describe started. A slow upload waits with its Need parts while another group's clean end and a collection go through the host, and its remaining bytes and end are then relayed. An untrusted author's attested recipe is refused before `Admit` | the host assembles before it checks the summary, compares NEW's limit alone, assembles or activates with an undecided grant diff, accepts a decision on a diff other than the one held, loses the hold at a hang-up, assembles before confirmation or on any confirmation, or releases the reservation before the Preparing row | launch.md "Publish and assemble", §14.3 |
| L49 `operator_request_identity`: after a host restart a retried Begin of an Installed D is answered Installed and starts no admission; other content under the same D is `Conflict`; an operator that hangs up mid-upload has its attempt released, and an identical Begin repeats it under a fresh attempt; a second D on the group reaches Preparing while Status of the first still reads Installed | the host keys a deploy by group instead of D, forgets D across a restart, accepts different content for a known D, keeps a dead connection's admission, or reuses its attempt | launch.md "The operator socket" |
| L50 `host_start` (launcher harness, crates/hirsel-core-driver/src/launcher: `host_restart_backoff`, `host_restart_wired`, `control_peer_is_the_host`; runtime: `operator_listener_is_private`): a host that ends 75 is started at 0 s, 0 s and 1 s within 2.5 s and the schedule doubles to 5 min and resets after a 5 min run; one that ends 74 or 64 is never started again; a same-UID process that is not the host does not get the control session; the inherited operator listener is adopted only for the socket it is bound at, and a connection from another UID is closed, never returned | the launcher restarts in a loop without backoff, restarts after a configuration failure, lets any local process take over the session, or the runtime adopts the listener for another path or skips the peer UID check | launch.md "Starting the host" |
| L51 `operator_register`: a Register sends `Bind` before the first release's `Admit`, whose approvals have no OLD; an upload cut off mid-registration is released and the same Register admits again under a fresh attempt with no OLD; a Begin to the registering group is `UnknownGroup` and nothing starts; the assembled release is Installed at activation 0, the attempt is released and a serving start at activation 0 runs it with the group's arguments and, for a recorded group, the recording named by incarnation‖generation whose pin names that assembly until exactly its retention ends; the same D again reads Installed and admits nothing, the same D with other content and a second group on the same store are Conflict; a failed admission or an unbindable listener records Failed, removes the group and sends `Close`, and the name registers again | the host admits before the listener is bound or against an empty OLD, lets a registering group be woken or deployed to, commits genesis at another activation or never starts it, drops a group's arguments, records without a durable pin or under a reused ID, admits a retried registration twice, lets two groups share a store, or leaves a failed registration's group or listener behind | launch.md "Registering a group" |
| L52 `deploy_drift_retry`: OLD's first drift aborts nothing and fails nothing: the row is Preparing at a fresh attempt, OLD is asked to export again as a retry naming it, after the export, prepare and import no candidate starts while the discarded one lives, its end leaves `Dirty(g)`, and the candidate then starts at gen 3 and the cutover is a retry naming the fresh attempt; a second drift records `Failed("drift")` at `Dirty(g)`, aborts OLD once and leaves no held answer | the host aborts on the first drift, reuses the drifted attempt or its candidate generation, starts the retry's candidate beside the live discarded one (which the launcher refuses), sends the retry with a fresh pause deadline, or retries without bound | launch.md "Hot deploy", §14.3 "Abort" |
| L53 `untrusted_source_admission` (v2.33): an untrusted author's source whose tree grants `Foreign` or `Exec`, and any source for its recording group, is `Denied` with no `Admit`; bytes that are no tree are `Invalid` with no `Admit`; a plain tree is sent to `Admit`, and the launcher's `AdmissionFailed(Uncompiled(d))` fails the request `Uncompiled` with exactly `d`. The launcher half (crates/hirsel-core-driver/src/launcher/source_laws.rs, `source_publishes_with_the_normal_checker`): a tree admits as the H that `hirsel publish` of the same files prints, twice; a type error is `Uncompiled` containing that command's diagnostic and adds no recipe to the code store | the host sends an untrusted Foreign, Exec or recording source to `Admit`, maps a refused compile to another class or drops its diagnostics; the launcher compiles under other stores than its own, admits something other than the publication, or records a recipe for refused source | launch.md "What an untrusted author submits", "Publish and assemble" |
| L54 `deploy_preview`: OLD exports entries 1, 2 and 4 at revision 1 and NEW exports 1 (revision 1), 2 (revision 2) and 3; the deploy's preview lists, sorted by identity, 1 unchanged, 2 changed, 3 added and 4 removed, and carries the prepare child's trigger report row for row. A prepare child that exits 0 without a report aborts OLD once and fails the deploy `prepare ended without its trigger report` | the host diffs entries by identity alone or by position, drops an entry only one side exports, or cuts over without the trigger report | launch.md "Deploy preview", §14.3 |
| `activation_window_recovers` (v2.32): a group added at activation 0 has its genesis start crash after the install. The probe at 0 is answered `PlaceCurrent(1)`, the group records 1, and the probe's end relaunches at once at 1, with no failing entry. That start's open records the store, and its clean report writes `Clean`. The starts are (1, 0), (2, 0), (3, 1). The same holds with a host restart before the crash is seen, with starts (2, 0), (3, 1) after it. A host restart after the adoption's commit and before the probe's end starts (3, 1). A start at 1 that is refused `PlaceCurrent(1)` too (another image's store) leaves the activation at 1, waits on backoff, and lists the group failing with a `NotCurrent` cause | the host drops the probe's `PlaceCurrent`, so the group fails on backoff at 0 until a deploy; it records the activation without the next open's proof, or adopts again from a nonzero activation; or it relaunches through backoff | §14.5 "Genesis" |
| `pin_offline_reader`: on a closed catalog, the offline reader answers the same Found as the live host before a pin expires, and Gone at its expiry, for a removed pin and for an unknown ID; a directory with no catalog is an error, never Gone | replay without a host reads a stale or wrong table, treats an expired or removed pin as live, or reports Gone when the catalog cannot be read | launch.md "Release recipe and host assembly" (replay) |

### 14.8 What this changes elsewhere, and the conflicts

| # | Existing rule | Conflict | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | v2.21 §13.5 "the directory does not wake an unloaded place" | the platform must wake one | Resolved: the directory is deleted (row 9), and every hosted place, in-process or launched, is woken by §14. |
| 2 | v2.21 §13.7 Durable Objects "Not taken: alarm wake-up" | §14 takes it | Resolved: §14.10 takes it; §13.7 is deleted with the directory. |
| 3 | v2.21 §13.6 PD4 `dir_reopen_keeps` | none | Ported as `local_reopen_keeps` (§14.9), where the host's wake runs the due trigger. |
| 4 | §12.1 `Ended = Stopped \| Forced(u64)` | the clean report needs `next_due`, and a stop during `Starting` cannot know it | `Stopped(Option[u64]) \| Unstarted \| Forced(u64)`. The drain's finish returns `Stopped(next_due)`, a stop during `Starting` returns `Unstarted`, and `main` exits 0 for both but reports only `Stopped`. Every match on `Stopped` (the directory, the fixture, `main`) changes with it. Pre-1.0, nothing migrates. |
| 5 | §12.1 "operate publishes at each phase change"; the watch channel of capacity 6 | the idle stop adds inputs to `operate` | The idle stop adds messages on `operate`'s own inbox, not phases. No new phase, no new `Status` publish, and the watch channel stays at six sends. |
| 6 | §12.2 "the preview opens no store" | the trigger scan needs the store's rows | The scan runs in the prepare child, which already opens the export and stages the migration. The manifest preview is unchanged. |
| 7 | ENDSTATE `storage-triggers` "after a long outage every trigger … runs once at the next open" | none | A wake is an open. Lateness under `Full` is T2's lateness. |
| 8 | ENDSTATE `storage-runs` "a drain … is the same cut: the next start is attempt n + 1" | idle stops would cut runs | §14.4's idle rule counts active runs, so an idle stop never cuts one. An operator stop still does, as before. |
| 9 | v2.21 §13 directory (several places in one process) vs the authority group (one store, one process) | a directory runs several groups in one process | The directory is not a tenant mechanism. **Done (v2.31):** it is superseded by the platform host and deleted; PD1 to PD4 are ported to the host's in-process launcher (§14.9). |
| 10 | rule 11 "no guard against our own implementation" | containment and the generation fence | Neither guards our own code. Containment bounds tenant code that is source-compiled but untrusted (T2-Q1). The fence orders reports from processes that really overlap during a cutover. |
| 11 | "no hidden cap changes an answer" (host rules) | the scan, the probe set and the queued wakes | The scan is exact or fails `Limit(triggers)`. Probes are all `Dirty` rows, which the invariant bounds, and nothing is truncated. A `Full` delay is reported in the host status and changes no trigger's result. |

### 14.9 In-process places (v2.31)

`local` (store/local.hirsel) is the platform host with an in-process launcher. A program that hosts first-party places in its own process, such as the harness gateway, uses it.

**Shape.** `local.local(config)` opens the host's catalog store at `root/host` and starts one actor that owns the `platform.Host` and every place task. The config names the place factory, the seed every start sees, `max_places`, the idle time each start gets, the wire sessions each place admits, the bytes each place may use and a watch channel. The actor answers the host's requests as a launcher would:
- `Hello` gets an inventory of the live place tasks; `Bind` is answered `Bound` with no listener (an in-process place takes its connections through `route`).
- `Start` of a serving child makes the group's workdir under the root and starts the factory's place on `Disk(root/workdir)` at `Current(activation)`, as a hosted child selects (launch.md "Activation"), with the host's idle time as `PlaceConfig.idle` and its session count as `PlaceConfig.max_sessions`. A repeated `Start` of the same child answers `Launched`; a second child for a group, or any start while the host stops, is refused `Held`. A non-serving start is refused: an in-process host has no deploy.
- `Stop` and `Kill` both drain the place. A task cannot be killed; `operate` cuts a drain only at its own deadline.
- `Retire` drains the group's task, and `Close` of a group retired with `Delete` removes its workdir.
- Deploy, admission and collection requests are answered as by a launcher that holds none (`Fenced`, `Collected(0)`, `AdmissionFailed(Unassembled)`).

The in-process launcher starts groups from one linked factory. Its catalog has
no deploy admission or retained assembly images for an install child to select.
It refuses an install launch's assembly selection as `Mismatch`. Genesis for
these groups runs on their first serving start at activation 0.

**Ends.** When the place reaches `Ready`, the actor checks its `Status.store_id` against the store the host recorded and reports `PlaceOpened`. A mismatch is reported `PlaceStoreMismatch` and the place is stopped. A start that fails `NotCurrent(Active)` is reported `PlaceCurrent`, with the probe's read when §14.3 asks for one. When `operate` returns, the actor reports the end with the child's `gen`: `Stopped(next_due)` is exit 0 with the report, `Unstarted` is exit 0 with none, and anything else is exit 1. (v2.32) The routes waiting on a group whose start failed are refused only after the host has applied the end, and only if the host did not start the group again at once (§14.5 "Genesis", "Failing and backoff"). So §14.4's clean-report rule applies unchanged. The listener's readable bit is "a route waits for this group", so a route that arrives during a drain relaunches the place.

**Operations.** `add(name)` adds a trusted, uncontained group with no listener and no limits; `remove(name)` retires it with `Delete` and answers after `Close`. `route(name)` wakes the group and answers with a lease on the place's `Status` once the host has applied its `PlaceOpened` and the place still serves; `peek` answers only a place that is already up and otherwise `Busy`. A lease does not hold the place up: the place's own §14.4 idle check, which counts sessions, decides. `unload(name)` is `platform.unload` and answers with the end. `stop` drains every place and closes the host.

**Fate-sharing.** In-process places share the host's process, so a fault in one ends all. This is allowed only for first-party code: every group `local` adds is trusted and uncontained, and a tenant group runs only through a process launcher (§14.8 row 9).

**Laws (ported from PD1 to PD4).**

| Law | Wrong implementation it catches | Oracle |
|---|---|---|
| `local_one_head`: concurrent first routes to one cold group give one child; both are answered by it | each route that finds no ready child starts one, so two heads run and the second cannot open the store | §14.3 |
| `local_route_during_unload`: a route during an unload waits for the drain and is answered by a fresh child | the route is answered with the draining child | §14.3, §14.4 "Unload" |
| `local_idle_unload`: an idle child stops by `operate`'s drain, ends `Stopped`, and its group is routed again only after the store is closed | the launcher reports the end when it asks the child to stop, so the next child finds the store held | §14.4 |
| `local_reopen_keeps`: a group routed again after an unload keeps its committed writes, and a trigger that came due while it was unloaded runs | the next child starts on a fresh store or a fresh activation | §14.1, §14.4 |
| `local_genesis_recovers` (v2.32): a group's store was activated by its genesis (version 1), and the host's catalog holds no activation for it. A task cannot be killed in-process, so the law builds the crash's durable state: it removes `root/host` and adds the group again. The next route is answered by a Ready child at activation 1 | the start at 0 is refused `NotCurrent(Active(1))`, the route is refused before the host acts, or the group never records 1, so it never serves without an operator | §14.5 "Genesis" |

### 14.10 Prior art (each source opened)

- **Cloudflare workerd** (e9a7b9c, src/workerd/server/alarm-scheduler.c++). One alarm row per actor, `_cf_ALARM(actor_id, scheduled_time)` (:55-60). At startup it loads every row into memory and arms a timer for each (:81-99). An alarm runs only once the clock reads at least its time, and it re-waits if woken early (:180-192). A failed alarm is retried with backoff, up to `RETRY_MAX_TRIES` (alarm-scheduler.h:54-66, .c++:247-263). **ADOPTED:** one durable due-time row per place, and the clock re-check. **ADAPTED:** a row is `Clean(next_due)` only after a clean stop, and the scan at startup reads only `Dirty` rows, bounded by `max_places`, where workerd holds every alarm in memory (its own TODO, :84-85). **NOT TAKEN:** engine retries. A Hirsel trigger's failure is recorded and consumed, and re-arming is the program's choice (ENDSTATE triggers).
- **Rivet** (de28d20, rivetkit-rust/packages/rivetkit-core/src/actor/). The actor pushes its next alarm to the engine before it sleeps (schedule.rs:902-916, :956-961). A new generation pushes again, and a stale alarm from an earlier generation "wakes the actor once with nothing due; it never drops a pending alarm" (schedule.rs:918-926). An actor may sleep only with no run handler, no connection and no HTTP request (sleep.rs:218-251). **ADOPTED:** sleep only when no run or connection is active (§14.4), and a generation on each start. **REPLACED:** Rivet tolerates a stale alarm, while §14 fences reports by generation and writes `Clean` only from a clean stop, so a crash leaves `Dirty` and a probe, never a lost wake.
- **Goose** (d5ae5bb). A single-process language with no durable scheduler, no places and no process host. **NOT TAKEN:** nothing to take.
